\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}

Example 7.3
.

Let
{\mathbf{E}}
be a small category, viewed as a loosely discrete strict double category
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}\mathrel{:=}{\mathbf{\mathbb{F}}}(\overline{{\mathbf{E}}})^{\prime}
, so that
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}=\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}=\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{n}
(
n\geq 0
). By direct computation,
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}}
(which, by
Proposition

5.6
, is the power of
{\mathbf{E}}\pitchfork{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}
) is representable and is equivalent to the pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}([{\mathbf{E}},{\mathbf{Set}}])
. By
Examples

4.2
and
6.2
, we have

{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}([{\mathbf{E}},{\mathbf{Set}}])\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}([{\mathbf{E}},{\mathbf{Set}}]))\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}})\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}(\mathbf{\mathbb{E}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})

whose underlying isomorphism of categories, by
Proposition

5.7
, recovers the symmetry between categories internal to presheaf categories and
\mathbf{Cat}
-valued presheaves.

\mathbf{Cat}([{\mathbf{E}},{\mathbf{Set}}])\cong{\mathbf{VDblCat}}(\mathbf{\mathbb{E}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\cong[{\mathbf{E}},\mathbf{Cat}]

Just as to every category
\mathbf{A}
, there is an associated hom-functor
\mathbf{A}({-},{-})\colon\mathbf{A}{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathbf{A}\to{\mathbf{Set}}
sending every pair of objects to the set of morphisms between them, to every pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, there is an associated lax hom-functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},{-})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}
sending every pair of objects to the set of tight morphisms between them, and every pair of loose morphisms to the set of cells between them.

Proposition 7.4

(
[
Par11
, §2.1]
)
.

For every pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, there is a lax hom-functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},{-})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}
.

Proof.

The action on objects and tight morphisms is given by
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}({-},{-})
and the action on loose morphisms and cells is given by
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}({-},{-})
together with the source and target projections, as in the following diagram. Below,
s_{{-},{-}}
and
t_{{-},{-}}
are postcomposition by
s
and
t
respectively.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Set}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Set}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Set}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}({-},{-})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t{}^{\mathrm{op}}\times t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}({-},{-})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}({-},{-})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{{-},{-}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{{-},{-}}}

For each pair of objects
A
and
A^{\prime}
, the unitor is given by the following functor.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(A,A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(A,A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}({\mathsf{I}}_{A},{\mathsf{I}}_{A^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(A,A^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{I}}_{{({-})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{{\mathsf{I}}_{A},{\mathsf{I}}_{A^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{{\mathsf{I}}_{A},{\mathsf{I}}_{A^{\prime}}}}

For each pair of composable pairs of loose morphisms
A{\mathrel{\to}^{p}}B{\mathrel{\to}^{q}}C
and
A^{\prime}{\mathrel{\to}^{p^{\prime}}}B^{\prime}{\mathrel{\to}^{q^{\prime}}}C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, the compositor is given by the following functor.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(A,A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}(p,p^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}(p,p^{\prime})\times_{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(B,B^{\prime})}{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}(q,q^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}(q,q^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(C,C^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(A,A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}(p\odot q,p^{\prime}\odot q^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}(C,C^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{p,p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({-})}\odot{({-})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{p,p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{p\odot q,p^{\prime}\odot q^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{p\odot q,p^{\prime}\odot q^{\prime}}}

That this forms a lax functor follows from coherence for the pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
.
∎

By transposing the hom-functor, we obtain a functor of virtual double categories as follows.

(3)

{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}

Since the underlying category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
is the category of presheaves on the category
\underline{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}
underlying
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, we automatically obtain a Yoneda lemma with respect to the objects and tight morphisms of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, given simply by the ordinary Yoneda lemma with respect to
\underline{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}
. However, we also have a Yoneda lemma with respect to the loose morphisms and cells of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
; the idea is contained in
[
Par11
, Theorem 4.4]
, but the following proposition is simpler than the theorem ibid., as it concerns cells in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
rather than cells in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})
.

Proposition 7.5

(Yoneda lemma)
.

Cells in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
of the following form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},A_{0})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},A_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

are in natural bijection with elements of
\Xi(p_{1}\odot\cdots\odot p_{n})
.

Proof.

By the Yoneda lemma for the category
\underline{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}
, the tight morphisms framing
\alpha
correspond to elements
a_{0}\in F(A_{0})
and
a_{n}\in F^{\prime}(A_{n})
. By definition (
Example

7.2
), the cell
\alpha
corresponds to a natural transformation,

{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},A_{1})}{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{2})\times_{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},A_{2})}\cdots\times_{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},A_{n-1})}{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{n})\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1}\odot\cdots\odot p_{n})

which, upon taking the component at
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
and applying the corresponding function at
1_{p_{1}},\ldots,1_{p_{n}}
, produces an element of
q(p_{1}\odot\cdots\odot p_{n})
. Naturality implies that this is the only possible assignment.
∎

Corollary 7.6
.

Every chain of representables
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1}),\ldots,{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{n})
in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
admits a weak composite
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1}\odot\cdots\odot p_{n})
.

Proof.

Taking
q={\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-},p_{1}\odot\cdots\odot p_{n})
in
Proposition

7.5
, we get a globular cell of the appropriate shape, which is weakly opcartesian by another application of
Proposition

7.5
.
∎

This gives us all the pieces needed to recover the Yoneda embedding of
[
Par11
, Theorem 4.8]
: as desired, it is obtained by transposing the hom-functor, and then applying the loose monads and modules construction.

Proposition 7.7
.

For each pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, there is a fully faithful functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
of virtual double categories, hence also a fully faithful normal functor
Y_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}},{\mathbf{Set}})
.

Proof.

As described above (
3
), the functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})
is obtained by transposing the lax-hom functor of
Proposition

7.4
. Full faithfulness on tight morphisms follows from full faithfulness of the Yoneda embedding
\underline{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{Set}}^{\underline{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}
, while full faithfulness on cells follows from
Proposition

7.5
. Therefore, by
Propositions

4.5
and
6.2
, we obtain a normal functor of virtual double categories
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}})\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})
, which is fully faithful by
Proposition

4.12
.
∎

Explicitly, the embedding
Y_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})
sends an object
A\in{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
to the image under
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
of the identity loose monad
A(1,1)
on
A
.
The Yoneda lemmas for
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})

[
Par11
, Theorems 2.3, 3.18 & 4.4]
thereby follow readily from the Yoneda lemmas for
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
. We leave this as an instructive exercise for the reader. (For a hint, note that the Yoneda lemma for lax functors invokes both the ordinary Yoneda lemma and
Proposition

7.5
; whereas the Yoneda lemma for multimodulations makes use of the property that cells in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})
are precisely cells in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
satisfying some additional properties.) We suggest this indicates that
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
may be the more fundamental of the two ‘Yoneda embeddings’ for double categories.

Remark 7.8
.

In
[
FM24
]
,
[
FM24
]
study a variant of the Yoneda embedding with respect to
normal
lax functors
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}
. However, under the universal property of the
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}
construction recalled in
Proposition

4.5
, their theory is entirely equivalent to
[
Par11
]
’s
[
Par11
]
. For instance their normal lax hom-functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}\times{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}

[
FM24
, Construction 3.4.1]
is obtained from
Proposition

7.4
by transposition and their Yoneda lemma
[
FM24
, Theorem 4.2]
follows from
[
Par11
]
’s likewise.

8.
The elements construction

The data of the exponential
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}

is expressed in terms of presheaves (of categories) and natural transformations therebetween, and we are inevitably led to try transferring this data across the correspondence between presheaves and discrete fibrations. This motivates the following definition.

That composition of cells is indeed associative and unital follows from uniqueness of liftings for the amnestic isofibrations.

Define
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
(for
levelwise discrete fibrations
) to be the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwAmIsof}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
whose objects and loose morphisms are the discrete fibrations over
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}
and
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}
respectively.

Remark 8.2
.

We introduced
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
by way of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwAmIsof}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
above to emphasise which properties of discrete fibrations are important in defining the virtual double category structure. However, in what follows, we will focus on
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
, since it is this construction that is relevant to the theory of
Section

7
. Let us simply remark that several of the properties below (e.g.
Lemmas

8.4
and
8.7
) also hold at the greater level of generality of amnestic isofibrations.

This definition is justified by the following, which establishes that
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is indeed the fibrational analogue of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
.

Proposition 8.3
.

For each pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, there is an equivalence of virtual double categories
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}\simeq{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
.

Proof.

The structure of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is obtained by transfer of structure across the correspondence between presheaves (and natural transformations therebetween), and discrete fibrations (and commutative squares therebetween), using that precomposition for presheaves corresponds to pullback for discrete fibrations.
∎

As is suggested by
Theorem

6.2
, it is instructive to consider loose monads in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
. These admit a particularly simple description in the special case in which
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is strict, by virtue of the fact that strict double categories are precisely monads in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})
(in contrast, pseudo double categories are merely pseudomonads, the consideration of which requires three-dimensional structure as in
[
CLPS22
]
). To explain this, we will need a couple of preparatory lemmas.

Lemma 8.4
.

For each strict double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, the virtual double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is isomorphic to the full sub-virtual double category of the slice
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
(
Definition

4.13
) spanned by the span morphisms whose components are discrete fibrations.

Proof.

When
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is strict, the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}m_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\odot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\odot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\odot}

Consequently, the lift appearing in the definition of composition in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is trivial, so that composition is precisely composition of spans via pullback.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a strict monoidal category. By
Lemma

8.4
, the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/(\Sigma\mathbf{A})
spanned by the span morphisms whose components are discrete fibrations is isomorphic to
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}(\Sigma\mathbf{A})
and hence, by
Proposition

8.3
, is equivalent to
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{\Sigma(\mathbf{A}{}^{\mathrm{op}})}
. We may restrict this equivalence on both sides to the one-object sub-virtual double categories on the terminal objects (i.e. the identity functor on the terminal category on one hand, and the terminal presheaf on the other), obtaining an equivalence of monoidal categories. By inspection of
Example

7.2
, the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{\Sigma(\mathbf{A}{}^{\mathrm{op}})}
on the terminal presheaf is precisely the delooping of the exponential multicategory
{\mathbf{Set}}^{\mathbf{A}{}^{\mathrm{op}}}
. As discussed in
Section

3.2
, this is precisely the convolution monoidal structure on
\mathbf{A}
. We thereby recover
[
EHL18
, Theorem 3.13]
. The authors of
[
EHL18
]
indicated that it would be desirable to extend their work to the setting in which
\mathbf{A}
is not assumed strict
[
EHL18
, §4]
, for which they anticipated working in a three-dimensional setting. Our
Lemma

8.4
suggests an alternative approach would be to work with (a full sub-virtual double category of)
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}(\Sigma\mathbf{A})
instead of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/(\Sigma\mathbf{A})
.

Definition 8.6
.

{\mathbf{DFib}}
is the full subcategory of the arrow category
\mathbf{Cat}^{\to}
spanned by the discrete fibrations.

Lemma 8.7
.

For each strict double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, the apex of the pullback
13
13

13

The reader should be warned that
[

, Example 3.23]
uses the notation
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}({\mathbf{DFib}})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
in place of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}({\mathbf{DFib}})_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
(though it is not a slice construction).

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}({\mathbf{DFib}})_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}

is isomorphic to the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
(

4.13
) spanned by the span morphisms whose components are discrete fibrations.

Proof.

Consider the following diagram. Since
\mathbf{Cat}^{\to}
is a power by the arrow category, hence a comma object, and
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}
preserves 2-limits (since
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}\mathrel{:=}{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}\circ{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}
, and both
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}
and
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}
preserve 2-limits), the right-hand square is also a comma diagram. By the pasting lemma for comma objects, the outer rectangle is a comma object, which is precisely the universal property of the slice
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
.

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\it{pb}}}

That this restricts to discrete fibrations as stated follows easily.
∎

Discrete fibrations of strict and pseudo double categories have been appeared before in the literature in several guises (perhaps the earliest allusion is
[
Par11
, Remark 3.10]
, though a definition is not given there). The following relates our virtual double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
with each of the notions appearing in the literature.

Theorem 8.8
.

Let
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
be a pseudo double category.

(8.8.(1))

For
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
strict, the virtual double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
of
[
Lam21
, Definition 3.24]
is equivalent to
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}))
.

(8.8.(2))

For
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
strictly normal, the category
\mathbf{DDblFib}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
of
[
CLPS22
, Definition 3.43 & (3.20)]
is equivalent to the underlying category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}))
.

(8.8.(3))

For
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
strict, the 2-category
\mathrm{D}\mathcal{F}\mathrm{ib}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
of
[
FM24
, Notation 5.2]
is equivalent to the underlying 2-category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}))
.

Proof.

(8.8.(1))

The virtual double category of discrete fibrations over a strict double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is defined by
[
Lam21
]
as
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}({\mathbf{DFib}})_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, which, by
Lemma

8.7
, is equivalent to the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
spanned by the span morphisms whose components are discrete fibrations. By
Lemma

8.4
, we have that
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is isomorphic to the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
spanned by the span morphisms whose components are discrete fibrations. Finally, we have
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat}))/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}ist}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
by
Proposition

4.16
, from which the equivalence follows by restricting to sub-virtual double categories.

(8.8.(2))

Explicitly, an object of
\mathbf{DDblFib}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is a strict functor of double categories
E\colon\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
such that
E_{0}\colon\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}
and
E_{1}\colon\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}
are discrete fibrations; and a morphism is a commutative triangle of strict functors of double categories. Now, it is clear that the unit and multiplication for a loose monad in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
, being defined via lifting the loose identities and loose composition in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, define a pseudo double category structure on
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, and that
E\colon\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}
thereby defines a strict functor. That the components are discrete fibrations is by definition. Similarly, a loose monad morphism in
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
is a strict functor, the data of the tight morphism determining the action on
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}
and the data of the cell determining the action on
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
.

(8.8.(3))

The 2-category of discrete fibrations over a strict double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is defined by
[
FM24
]
as the underlying 2-category of the full sub-virtual double category of
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}(\mathbf{Cat})/{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
spanned by the spans whose components are discrete fibrations. Therefore, the equivalence follows as in (1).
∎

A virtual double category of discrete fibrations over a
pseudo
double category has not previously been defined in the literature.
Theorem

8.8
justifies us in defining
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
to be the virtual double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}))
. Having set up our definitions, we immediately obtain a correspondence between presheaves on, and discrete fibrations over, a pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
.

Corollary 8.9
.

For each pseudo double category
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
, there is an equivalence of virtual double categories
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\simeq{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
.

Proof.

From
Proposition

8.3
, we have
{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}\simeq{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
, whereupon applying
{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}
gives
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}})\simeq{\mathbf{\mathbb{M}od}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}))\cong{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
by
Theorems

6.2
and
8.8
.
∎

When
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is strict,
Corollary

8.9
recovers
[
Lam21
, Theorem 4.14]
; the underlying equivalence of 2-categories, together with
Proposition

4.5
, recovers
[
FM24
, Theorem 6.13]
. When
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is a strictly normal pseudo double category, the underlying equivalence of categories recovers
[
CLPS22
, Theorem 3.45]
. When
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is a pseudo double category, the full faithfulness of the functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\to{\mathbf{DFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
recovers
[
Par11
, Theorems 3.8 & 3.11 & Proposition 3.9]
. Finally, when
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is a bicategory,
Corollary

8.9
recovers an unpublished correspondence due to Cockett–Niefield–Wood
[
Nie12
]
.

Remark 8.10
.

We note that the correspondence proven by
[
CLPS22
]
permits the base
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
to vary. Our view is that it is the correspondence with the fixed base that is primary; we explain briefly how one might go about obtaining the correspondence for a varying base from the correspondence for a fixed base. There are 2-functors
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({-}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\colon{\mathbf{DblCat}}_{l}\to\mathbf{NVDblCat}
and
{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({-})\colon{\mathbf{DblCat}}_{l}\to\mathbf{NVDblCat}
, which are 2-naturally equivalent. Our expectation is that such functors should admit an elements construction (necessarily of a three-dimensional nature). Thereafter, applying the elements construction to the equivalence
{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({-}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})\simeq{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({-})
would produce the desired correspondence for a varying base.

We conclude with some simple but useful consequences of the correspondence between presheaves and discrete fibrations.

Proposition 8.11
.

The embedding
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}({-}_{2},{-}_{1})\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}}^{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op\,t}}}
corresponds under
Proposition

8.3
to the functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}vwDFib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
sending a loose morphism
p\colon A{\mathrel{\to}}B
to the following span morphism.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}/A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}/p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}/B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s/p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t/p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

Consequently, the embedding
Y_{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{L}ax}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathbf{\mathbb{S}pan}})
corresponds under
Corollary

8.9
to the functor
{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{D}Fib}}({\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}})
each object
A\in{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
to the projection functor from the slice double category
\pi_{A}\colon{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}/A\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
.

Proof.

The equivalence of
Proposition

8.3
is levelwise, and so the results follow from the corresponding result for categories by unwrapping the definition of the embeddings in
Proposition

7.7
.
∎

Corollary 8.12
.

A discrete fibration
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
of pseudo double categories corresponds under the equivalence of
Corollary

8.9
to a representable presheaf if and only if its
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}
admits a terminal object whose identity loose morphism is terminal in
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
.

Proof.

Following
Proposition

8.11
, a discrete fibration
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}
is representable if and only if each component discrete fibration
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}
corresponds to a representable presheaf – i.e.
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
admit terminal objects – and furthermore, the representing object for
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{1}
is the loose identity on the representing object for
\mathbf{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{\mathbb{A}}}_{0}
. Thus, the characterisation for discrete fibrations of double categories follows directly from the characterisation for discrete fibrations of categories, applied componentwise.
∎

For instance, in combination with
Proposition

7.5
,
Proposition

8.11
extends
[
FM24
, Theorem 7.4]
from strict double categories to pseudo double categories, while
Corollary

8.12
extends
[
FM24
, Theorems 7.7 & 7.9]
likewise.

Remark 8.13
.

For simplicity, we have chosen to focus here on the elements correspondence for tight presheaves and discrete fibrations of double categories, but note that precisely the same techniques apply to obtain a correspondence between tight distributors and two-sided discrete fibrations of pseudo double categories.
